# Board

Open work that outlives a run. Priority: P1 blocks a user outcome, P2 degrades one, P3 is hygiene.
A row closes only with a receipt (commit, command output or test name).
A status starts with its state, one of `open`, `in` (progress or review), `done`, `closed`, `released`, `fixed`, `superseded`, `decided`, `filed`, `transferred`, `handed`: readers of the shared backlog take the first word. A row closed in its last note starts with `closed <date> (receipt at the end)`. A `|` inside a cell is written `\|`. `scripts/check_docs.py` checks all three.

| SB-25 | P1 | A session stopped on a limit continues on another account without the operator. Today Claude Code stops on a usage or spend limit (`429`, *individual spend limit*, *continuing automatically at <reset>*) and waits for the reset or for the operator to type «продолжай», even when Switchboard has already detected the limit and activated an account with quota (`monitor::scan_limits`, `a_limit_error_in_claude_code_switches_with_quota_to_spare`). Research, then plan as a deliberate feature (not an incidental fallback, AGENTS.md): (1) whether the stopped session takes the new account without a restart (SB-06); (2) the wake channel — a cross-session message to the stopped session, `claude --resume <session>` in its own terminal, or an Observatory handoff with reason `limit` accepted by a fresh session from workflow memory (PB-137, M9); (3) guards: one wake per limit event, never a replayed prompt or a half-done tool call, never back into the exhausted account, an organisation spend limit shared by every seat is not a per-account quota, an operator switch to turn it off; (4) a scenario and evidence on a live session | operator request 2026-10-03: a subagent and its session stopped on *individual spend limit* until the 02:40 reset | **closed** 2026-10-14 — absorbed: automatic hand-over along a chain (SB-73, v0.6.8), a limit stop restarted at once on fresh capacity (SB-115, v0.6.28); the manual path is `switchboard continue` (v0.6.2) |

| SB-57 | P3 | The Claude sign-in source flips `refused` → `available` within seconds, 43 times in the installed app's log on 2026-10-05 (`credential_source` events): a probe that lands while Claude Code rewrites its Keychain item reads as refused and backs off. | SB-105 | P2 | A ready update can wait forever: once an update is `ready` the check loop stops (`updates::check` returns early while `inner.ready` is set), so a later release is never seen, and the only way out is the idle watch (SB-77), which logs nothing while it waits. Observed on the operator's Mac 2026-10-12: the app running since 2026-10-10 17:34 UTC logged `update_install installed` / `update_check ready` at 23:36 UTC, then no `update_check` and no `update_restart` for 35 h; 0.6.17/0.6.18 unseen; the bundle on disk 0.6.16. Which idle input stayed false (window visible, a managed request in flight, a sign-in held in memory) is not observable from outside — a fact to find, not assumed. Also present: an orphan sign-in folder `logins/<id>/` from 2026-10-08 (pre-SB-65, no `.login.json`, its Terminal pid gone). Fix: (1) the idle watch logs, at most hourly, which input kept it from starting (codes only); (2) while an update waits, keep checking every 6 h and replace the ready one with a newer release (macOS: install the newer into the bundle); (3) a test on a fake clock for both; (4) confirm the orphan folder is swept at start since SB-103. The lifecycle broker cannot restart it either (`in_use_by_person`, `not_started_by_broker` — the login item started it) | operator Mac check 2026-10-12 | **done** 2026-10-12 (branch `fix/sb-105-update-waits`): checks go on while an update waits and a newer release replaces it (`updates::replaces`); `update_waiting {reason}` at once then hourly (`idle_blocker`, `IdleReport`, `Runtime::busy_reason`); `Updates::swap` keeps a restart out of a bundle swap; the pre-SB-65 leftover is swept (`a_sign_in_home_from_before_its_journal_is_swept_once_its_terminal_ended`). CONTRACTS → A waiting update never waits forever. Ships with the next release; the operator's copy still needs one restart by hand to leave 0.6.16 |

| SB-151 | P1 | An idle update restart left no app running: on 2026-10-20 the 0.6.53 app logged `update_restart drained` (34105 ms), `update_restart launch: background` at 05:31:29 and `hard_exit` at 05:31:39 UTC, and no process came up until the lifecycle broker started it at 05:52 (owner, proxy and control listener down for 20 minutes; managed sessions without their route) | SB-149 installation, 2026-10-20 | done in 0.6.57 (published 2026-10-20 15:52 UTC): the relaunch is no longer on the exit path. Windows is unchanged (the NSIS installer relaunches). Test `the_relaunch_helper_starts_the_new_copy_only_after_the_old_one_ended`. down from 0.6.83's 69.3 s `renewal`. Left: `renew_live` holds the transaction across the network grant; the design note this row asks for starts there · 2026-10-30: cause of the hourly `source_sync` holds found: every sync ended with `Runtime::invalidate_current`, which forgot the sign-in watch, so each sync (every 180 s) read both sign-ins again (on macOS a `security` call) inside the transaction, against LC-04; the idle-hour test used its own watch and never saw it (with the forget wired it counts 40 reads an hour). **Built** on branch `fix/sb-163-source-sync-reads`: the reads are taken before the transaction (`prime_live_sources`, off the async worker), the watch is forgotten only after a live renewal that may have written the sign-in, `NativeSources::forget` lets the test see it; `an_idle_hour_on_a_fake_clock_stays_inside_the_budget` fails without the fix. Close only together with Fabric Dashboards' daily count of "no answer within 10 s" after a day on 0.6.81 · 2026-10-25 on 0.6.82 (21:59 to 11:40 UTC): still not quiet: `control_slow` `project_suggest` 19 (up to 22.5 s), `sessions` 12 (36.9 s), `recovery` 7 (29.1 s), `current_accounts` 1 (37.8 s); `vault_slow` 4 (11.9 s); no `transaction_held`; hotfix 0.6.83 ([audit 2026-10-25](../reports/2026-10-25-audit-and-plan/README.md)) · 0.6.83 (`2b41721`, branch `release/0.6.83`): the cause found on 2026-10-25 (load 89-100, 11 GB swap; `snapshot`, `recovery`, `sessions` 5-52 s, `source_sync` held 129 s of which `renew_live` 91 s): `Store::adopt_refreshed_for` read every saved OAuth account's Keychain item with the store's lock held, and every Keychain read waits behind one process lock (`keychain_macos::quiet`) with no deadline. The list is now read under the lock and the reads run without it; `adopting_a_renewal_never_holds_the_account_list_across_keychain_reads` waited 724 ms before. Measure `control_slow` again on the installed 0.6.83 · **0.6.83 published** 2026-10-26 00:47 UTC (`1de6648`, run 40245506270); the day's measurement on the installed copy follows · **Day on 0.6.83** (switchboard.log, owner started 2026-10-26T01:22:41Z, read 2026-10-27 02:27 local; load 36/70/67 at the read, 89-100 on 10-25; swap 33-35 of 35 GB from about 18:00 UTC): not quiet. `control_slow` `sessions` 61 (max 108.8 s), `recovery` 34 (104.6 s), `snapshot` 4 (16.6 s), `project_suggest` 7 (15.1 s), `handovers` 1 (13.9 s), `recovery_hook` 1 (5.3 s); `vault_slow` 9 (max 4.5 s); `transaction_held` 18. On 10-25 (whole UTC day, 0.6.82): `sessions` 128 (165.3 s), `recovery` 89 (135.8 s), `snapshot` 14 (41.5 s), `project_suggest` 36 (35.6 s); `vault_slow` 19 (19.3 s); `transaction_held` 23. Counts fell by half or more, the longest `vault_slow` from 19.3 to 4.5 s (H-1 holds), but reads outside a transaction still waited past 5 s, so the row stays open. **Next cause, two:** (1) `renewal`'s `store` phase held the store's lock 69.3 s at 18:42:09 (also 16.7 s at 03:39, 14.9 s at 23:35), and `Operation::Sessions` and `Recovery` begin with `store.snapshot()` (`crates/switchboard-runtime/src/lib.rs:4263`), which waits for that lock: `recovery` 63 to 64 s and `sessions` at 18:42 to 18:43 match it; the store phase writes the renewed token (a Keychain write) under the lock, the sibling of SB-206; (2) 21:20 to 21:34 `sessions` 32.8 and 108.8 s and `recovery` 104.6 s with no owner event within 5 s of them, while the machine swapped 34 of 35 GB and Claude Code reaped background shells for memory: host starvation, nothing in Switchboard held anything. Next step: move the Keychain write of a renewal out of the store's lock (with SB-206), and log the store lock's own wait in `control_slow` (`lock_ms`) so (1) and (2) are told apart from the log alone · 2026-10-29, day on 0.7.0 (2026-10-28 09:42 to 2026-10-29 07:38 UTC, the installed app's log): 14 `control_slow`, longest `sessions` 8.8 s with `lock_ms` 5.1 s (03:38:09, four seconds after a `source_sync` whose `sync` phase held 8.6 s), the rest under 7.7 s with `lock_ms` under 0.7 s; none past 10 s (0.6.83's day: 32.8 and 108.8 s). With `lock_ms` the two causes now read apart: one wait on the store's lock behind `source_sync`, the others host time outside every lock. Stays open for the lock wait behind `source_sync` · 2026-11-02, three days on 0.7.2 (2026-10-30 13:49 to 2026-11-02 12:13 UTC, one owner run): 78 `control_slow`, every one `usage` with `lock_ms` 0, at 5 to 16 s (23 at 10 s, 26 at 15 s, one 25 s), each with a `usage_check` `unreachable` or `rejected` beside it: quota checks a caller asked of the provider waiting on its network timeout, not the owner; no `sessions`, `recovery` or `handovers` past the threshold. **Closed 2026-11-02**: no read waits on the store's lock any more (0.6.83 `adopt_refreshed_for`, 0.7.2 SB-163); a slow provider answer is SB-48's and the caller's timeout · 2026-11-03: the installed app's log has 881 `control_slow` lines from 2026-10-23 to 2026-11-02 23:35Z (`sessions` 366, up to 165 s; `recovery` 269; `project_suggest` 122; `usage` 80; `handovers` 9, up to 19.5 s; 141 on 2026-11-02, when the disk had 5 GB free at 21:20 UTC by the coordinator's count) and none since 0.7.5 started (2026-11-03 00:46Z) to 13:40Z: watch one more week before closing |

| SB-179 | P2 | SB-163 T-2: a token grant leaves the owner transaction: it runs under a per-account refresh lock and the transaction is taken only to store the result, checked against the credential generation it started from | [owner-transaction-2026-10-23](../research/owner-transaction-2026-10-23.md) T-2 · goal: reliable-work | done in 0.6.77: `refresh::ensure_fresh_with` takes the owner transaction for the checks and the reservation (the token marked in flight), not for the grant, and again to store the successor; the drain stores kept successors (`adopt_all_kept`). Built before a day of T-1 phases: the grant is network time by construction, and the reservation under the transaction is what keeps activation and renewal apart, whatever the phases show · live: from the 0.6.77 owner start (2026-10-23 12:09:53 UTC) to 18:37 UTC, no `transaction_held` and no `control_slow` line (266 `transaction_held` before it in the same log) · **correction 2026-10-24:** in 0.6.77 and 0.6.78 the renewal took the lock without `hold`, so it could not log `transaction_held`; the "no line since 12:09:53" above counts an instrument that could not fire for it. The 0.6.79 review (unit, seam and product readings) found three defects of this change, fixed with tests: the drain did not wait for a quota check's grant, so its new token could die with the process (`the_drain_stores_the_token_a_quota_checks_renewal_returns_during_it`); a launch under the transaction got Transient and the old token while the background renewal was in flight (`an_account_whose_renewal_is_in_flight_is_not_switched_to`); a successor named by its owner was filed over a sign-in stored during the grant (`a_grant_runs_outside_the_transaction_and_a_sign_in_stored_meanwhile_stands`, now with the owner named); and a rejected grant marked such a sign-in (`a_rejected_grant_does_not_mark_a_sign_in_stored_meanwhile`). Renewal spans log again (`renewal`, `usage_renewal`) |

Done when: (a) a home Switchboard launches gets the newest sign-in of each MCP server from the person's other homes (the entry with its `clientId` and its `mcpOAuthClientConfig`), merged into that home's item under Claude Code's own locks, never the account's `claudeAiOauth`; (b) a renewal Claude Code writes in one home is carried to the others at the next launch or source sync, newest `expiresAt` wins; (c) no value enters a log, IPC answer or Git; (d) synthetic Keychain fixtures only (`AGENTS.md`), with a test that a home whose item would ask for access is skipped, not prompted. | SB-410 | P3 | `control::tests::read_only_requests_answer_while_a_slow_keychain_read_holds_the_keychain` timed out once under load: the full gate on a build machine at `956bf9d7` (2026-11-17, load average 12 to 16 from another session's gate) failed with “recovery waited for the held Keychain” (`crates/switchboard-runtime/src/control.rs:973`, 5 s timeout), while the test alone passed three times in 0.1 s and the whole `switchboard-runtime` suite passed right after. Find what `Operation::Recovery` waits on when the blocking pool is busy, or give the test a bound that holds under a loaded machine without hiding a real stall | that run's gate log (`test control::tests::read_only_requests_answer_while_a_slow_keychain_read_holds_the_keychain ... FAILED`); PR #810 | released in 0.7.116 (PR #816 `f5e2c77e`, built 2026-11-17; tag `v0.7.115` was never published). Cause: a real stall, not a slow machine. `sign_in_known` (`recovery`, `monitor status`, the attention list, chains) answered from its cache only while no verdict had changed since; a usage check's rejection (`reject`) or a renewal refused (`remember_dead`) moved the generation, and the next read-only request read every ended account's credential itself, behind the held Keychain. It now answers from the verdicts alone in that case (each account keeps the answer computed for its lineage, a new one needs a sign-in, a removed account is left out), caches nothing, and the background pass settles it with the vault; test `refresh::tests::a_read_only_answer_after_a_new_verdict_reads_no_credential` counts no read. Review round 1: with no answer yet (no owner, or before its first pass) it still reads the vault, since the refused lineages are a journal kept across restarts (`with_no_answer_yet_a_replaced_lineage_is_read_from_the_vault`). Left as known (review round 2): the vault-free path keeps only the last certain answer, so while a Keychain read keeps failing and a new verdict arrives, accounts with a journalled lineage may read as needing a sign-in until the next pass (30 s or less); display and guidance only, every switching, failover, fallback and claim path still reads the vault |

| SB-413 | P1 | A restart in place lost the session's output on Linux: the hosted session's output reader read the old process's end (EIO) just before the next process started, waited for the session's lock while the next process was spawned under it, then found the restart over and stopped, so viewers saw nothing of the next process and its exit only when it ended; the test `a_restart_in_place_keeps_the_viewers_and_runs_the_next_program_on_the_same_terminal` waited forever. | SB-597 | P3 | Groups in the owner's priority order (Fabric board request `3fc87214`, 2026-11-20; the owner's words relayed by Fabric's CEO session `53895bdc`: «В раздел группы это не приоритетное, потом … как чуть освободимся … но добавь»). The Groups page gets an order mode *Priority* with the same model as Fabric's order of projects: the first order comes from each group's last interaction (its last session or proxied request; a group with none, by the time it was added), then the owner drags a group and the order is kept for good; a new group takes the first place. Today groups are listed by name (each write sorts `candidate.projects` by `name`, `crates/switchboard-core/src/projects.rs`) and nothing keeps a person's order, and a group stores no time it was added. The order is saved with the groups in `accounts.json` (a schema bump), carried in backups and settings sync, and `switchboard --json project list` and MCP `switchboard_projects` answer each group's position (a field such as `order`), so Fabric reads it and keeps no copy of its own of the order of projects inside one group. This is not the feed's priority (SB-419, which ranks fed groups for ready accounts) and changes no routing or lending. The model is agreed with Fabric when its wave-plan row (filters and sorting of projects) comes up; no deadline. | board request 3fc87214 | open |
